# HaKorem Farms Platform: Assumptions

Every assumption made in place of an answer, dated, with who needs to confirm it and by when. An assumption stays here until it is confirmed (and the status says by whom) or replaced by a decision in an ADR.

Statuses: **Open** (nobody has confirmed it), **Confirmed** (the named person agreed), **Replaced** (an ADR or a later answer supersedes it).

"Needed before" is the build session that cannot finish correctly without the answer.

## Owner's answers recorded on 2026-11-13

The owner answered the open questions in the shared brief with "everything else on the brief's defaults", and approved three flagged items with conditions. These are recorded as confirmed.

| ID | Assumption | In place of | Confirmed by | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A-001 | Build multi-location from day one and run a single location | Number of locations at launch | Owner | Confirmed |
| A-002 | Investors hold equity in units; profit retention is 40 percent and owner-configurable | Number of investors, equity or loan, retention rate | Owner | Confirmed |
| A-003 | Live animals are not bought directly online. The storefront takes a request to buy, which becomes a quote | Whether live animals are sold online | Owner | Confirmed |
| A-004 | The business holds one mobile money account and one bank account. Cash on delivery is live at launch with driver reconciliation | Accounts held; cash on delivery | Owner | Confirmed |
| A-005 | No existing records are migrated. The accountant enters an opening balance journal on day one | Records to migrate, opening balances | Owner | Confirmed |
| A-006 | Domain `hakoremfarms.com`: storefront on the apex, console on `app.`, API on `api.` | Domain and subdomains | Owner | Confirmed |
| A-007 | A staging server on the smallest plan exists from the start | Staging from the start | Owner | Confirmed |
| A-008 | The order core is built at the start of B9, and B9 is two sessions: B9a order core and payments, B9b point of sale, shifts and offline sync (ADR-0026) | The schedule as written | Owner | Confirmed |
| A-009 | Investor distributions are allocated by unit-days, with largest-remainder rounding on integer pesewas. The contribution day counts; the withdrawal day does not; actual calendar days in Africa/Accra (ADR-0028) | The meaning of "weighted average shareholding" | Owner | Confirmed |
| A-010 | Tax is modelled as several named levies per category, each with its own base and reclaimable flag, seeded at zero where a levy does not apply. Produce, live animals and eggs are seeded zero-rated (ADR-0020) | VAT status and which levies apply | Owner, for the shape and the seed | Confirmed |

| A-060 | 2026-11-13 | In PostgreSQL 17, a session-level advisory lock taken before `BEGIN` makes a second serializable transaction wait and then succeed, whereas `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(...)` as the first statement inside the transaction does not, because that statement takes the snapshot before it blocks (ADR-0005) | A two-session test | B2 | Confirmed by test, B2, 2026-11-14: `test/integration/platform/advisory-lock.integration.spec.ts` passes both halves on PostgreSQL 17.6 (the second transaction waits and succeeds; with `pg_advisory_xact_lock` first it fails with 40001) |
| A-061 | 2026-11-13 | A receipt printed offline with a provisional reference, followed by an official number at sync, is acceptable to the Ghana Revenue Authority (ADR-0015). Checked on 2026-11-13: no source confirms it. GRA guidance says a taxpayer whose system goes offline tells GRA within 24 hours and uploads the offline transactions afterwards, and that certified E-VAT software can issue receipts offline for 24 hours (`docs/security/COMPLIANCE.md`). Still open | Accountant, then GRA | B9b | Open |
| A-062 | 2026-11-13 | Whether electronic invoice certification applies, and whether it totals tax at document level. Applies if A-033 finds the business registered | Accountant | B8 | Open |
| A-063 | 2026-11-13 | Ghana's Data Protection Act permits retaining invoices and receipts for the statutory period despite a deletion request. A permission leaves a role by soft deletion of its `iam.role_permission` link, with actor and reason, not the hard delete `docs/data-model/iam.md` described: ADR-0024 gives the application role no DELETE on a MASTER table; adding it back restores the link. No operation deletes a role. Every change is audited as `PERMISSION_CHANGE` and published as `iam.RoleChanged`. **Approvals:** the Owner, Manager and Accountant hold `iam.approval.read` and `iam.approval.cancel.own`; a later module that makes another role a maker grants them. Deciding needs `iam.approval.read` and the policy's approver permission at the request's branch, plus a step-up when the policy's `requires_step_up` is set (the policy flag is now enforced, beside the permission's own flag). Only the maker withdraws a pending, unlapsed request (`CANCELLED`, with the reason in `decision_reason`); the owning module's `onRejected` runs for a withdrawal as for a rejection. A pending request past `expires_at` shows as `EXPIRED`; nothing yet writes that status (a sweep job is B13's). `subject.number` is null until the document registry carries numbers (B8), and `evidenceFileId` until the file store (B04e continued 2). | A-123 | 2026-11-15 | The customer realm and password reset (FR-IAM-019, FR-IAM-020, CR-004 point 3; migration 0032). **Registration** takes a Ghana mobile number only (+233 and nine digits, else 400 `NOT_GHANA_PHONE`), and answers 201 `VERIFICATION_REQUIRED` whether or not the phone has an account: a known phone gets an SMS notice, no second account, and a challenge with no code, so no guess can pass it. An email another customer uses is left off the new account rather than refused, so the answer reveals no email. A customer account can sign in only once its phone is verified (`iam.user_account.phone_verified_at`); `loginCustomer` before that answers `VERIFICATION_REQUIRED` with a new code. Customer accounts made before migration 0032 count as verified at creation. Codes are six digits, ten minutes, five attempts (the sixth is refused even if right), stored as an HMAC beside the challenge. **Reset** answers 202 alike for every identifier; an active account with a phone gets a single-use token by SMS, valid thirty minutes, replacing any earlier one; an email-only account gets nothing until the email channel (B11). A wrong, used or expired token is 422 `RESET_TOKEN_INVALID`, `RESET_TOKEN_USED` or `RESET_TOKEN_EXPIRED`. A reset revokes every session, clears the lockout and, for a customer, verifies the phone (the token reached it). Five registrations per phone and five reset requests per identifier in fifteen minutes, fail closed, beside the per-address group. **Delivery:** IAM sends codes straight to the SMS port after commit and does not wait (no code in an outbox payload, ADR-0009); `MESSAGING_MODE` defaults to `capture` and must be `live` in production. **Events:** `iam.UserCreated` (realm, marketing consent and its time; no name, phone or email) at registration, `iam.PhoneVerified` on verification. Linking the account to an `mdm.customer` record, and storing the consent there, waits for the storefront account (B7). The audit trail records `PASSWORD_RESET_REQUESTED` with no actor when no account matches. No seeded customer account: the seed cannot read an SMS code, and B7 seeds storefront customers. New codes `VERIFICATION_CODE_INVALID`, `RESET_TOKEN_INVALID`, `RESET_TOKEN_USED`, `RESET_TOKEN_EXPIRED` | Owner | B7 | Open |

| A-126 | 2026-11-15 | Product photographs (FR-DOC-005, NFR-005; migrations 0036 and 0037, ADR-0045). **Variants:** the contract's names keep their meaning at FR-DOC-005's widths: `thumb` 320, `card` 640, `full` 1280 pixels, never wider than the original, the aspect ratio kept (the contract's examples showed 160, 480 and 1200). Each width is one `ImageVariant` with `url` (WebP) and `fallbackUrl` (JPEG) and both sizes in bytes (contract 1.13.0, additive); `platform.stored_file_variant` holds one row per width and format, PK (`file_id`, `variant`, `content_type`). **Budgets:** "200 KB" is read as 200,000 bytes, for `card`; `thumb` and `full` keep its bytes per pixel (50,000 and 800,000). Quality steps 80, 70, 60, 50, 40 until a rendition fits; a photograph that fits at none, cannot be decoded, or has more than 50 million pixels is recorded FAILED with the reason and never served. **When:** variants are rendered in the worker's scan, after ClamAV says CLEAN and before the verdict is recorded, so a CLEAN photograph always has them; storage that cannot be reached leaves it PENDING. **Who:** attaching and removing need `mdm.product.update` (Owner, Manager); attaching someone else's upload also needs `platform.file.read.any`. A file of another use, or none, is 422 `REFERENCE_NOT_FOUND` on `/fileId` (it says nothing about evidence files); not CLEAN is 422 `FILE_NOT_CLEAN`; already in the gallery is 422 `DUPLICATE_VALUE`. **Gallery:** with no `sortOrder` a photograph goes last; a gallery change publishes `mdm.ProductChanged` with `images` changed and does not move the product's `version`, so an editor's open form is not invalidated; removing is a soft delete with the reason "Removed from the product gallery", and the variants stay on the public origin. **Origin:** `MEDIA_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, required for the API in staging and production; in development and test the public bucket's path-style URL, readable by key through a bucket policy and never listable. **Seed:** no photograph is seeded: the seed would need the scanner running and real photographs, so the seeded catalogue has empty galleries until the owner's photographs are uploaded | Owner | B7 (the storefront shows the gallery) | Open |

| A-128 | 2026-11-15 | MDM imports and exports (FR-MDM-019, FR-MDM-020, SEC-009; migrations 0038 to 0041, ADR-0046). **Who:** the Owner and Manager hold `mdm.import.read`, `mdm.import.create` and `mdm.import.apply`; the Owner, Manager and Accountant hold `mdm.export.create`; the Accountant also reads the job list, which shows exports. Applying also needs the entity's own create or update permission for the rows that create or change records, and exporting the entity's read permission, so an import is never a way round them. **Step-up:** every export needs a fresh step-up, as the contract's `x-requires-step-up: true` on `createExportJob` and ADR-0016 already say. The bulk threshold is 100 rows (FR-MDM-020 and SEC-009; SA-02's 1,000 is the report threshold of RPT's `rpt.report.export.bulk` and is left open for the owner): an export of more than 100 rows is recorded with `bulk: true` in its `EXPORT` audit event, for B12's `BULK_EXPORT` item; it does not waive the step-up below it, the more cautious reading. **Dry run:** runs inside `createImportJob`, not in the worker, on a CLEAN `IMPORT` file read outside any transaction; at most 5,000 records (header excluded), so one request can check them; the job is `VALIDATED` with its row errors, or `FAILED` (errors on row 1) when the file cannot be read as the entity's sheet (unreadable, no header, an unknown, repeated or missing column, no rows, too many rows). `UPLOADED` is never stored. Blank rows are skipped and rows keep their spreadsheet numbers (the header is row 1). A row error names the row, the column and the rule, and never echoes a personal value (a customer number or tier code may appear). **Apply:** only a `VALIDATED` import with no row error; the rows are checked again inside the one serializable transaction, and if the database has moved so that a row fails, nothing is written, the new errors are added to the job (still `VALIDATED`, with fewer valid rows) and the answer is 422 `IMPORT_NOT_VALIDATED`. **Customers:** columns `customerNo`, `kind`, `name`, `phone`, `email`, `tier`; a blank `customerNo` creates a customer (kind and name required, tier RETAIL by default), a number changes that customer, where a blank cell changes nothing (an import never erases a value) and the kind cannot change; no TIN, address or consent column (restricted or needing the customer's own act). Each row goes through `PartiesAdmin`'s create or update in the import's transaction, so each is audited as by hand. Export columns are the same, live customers only, anonymised ones left out. **Exports:** written as XLSX (every cell a string in the Text format) or CSV (UTF-8 with a byte order mark, CRLF; a cell starting `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR gets a leading apostrophe, which reading the CSV back removes); registered as a private `EXPORT` file (a new purpose, CONFIDENTIAL, no upload accepted), PENDING like any file, so its link comes from `createFileSignedUrl` (five minutes, logged) once the worker's scan has passed it, rather than in the response: ADR-0044 keeps every file PENDING until scanned. The file is named `<entity>-export-<UTC date>.<xlsx|csv>`. **Job branch:** `mdm.import_job.branch_id` is the head office branch (master data is not branch-scoped), as for price list versions. **Entities:** customers are built now; products, suppliers, locations and price list entries are B04e continued 5's (A-129); `OPENING_STOCK` writes stock, which INV owns, so its dry run and apply wait for B5, and until then both operations answer 422 `IMPORT_ENTITY_NOT_AVAILABLE` for those entities | Owner | B04e continued 5 (the remaining sheets), B5 (opening stock), B12 (the exception report) | Open; the OPENING_STOCK sheet is built in B05 continued 4 (A-148, ADR-0050) |

The most conservative reading throughout. **Common:** as the customer sheet (A-128): columns matched without regard to case, a blank cell on a changing row changes nothing (an import never erases a value), each row written through the entity's own service in the import's transaction (`ProductCatalogue.createIn`/`updateIn`, `ReferenceDataAdmin.createSupplierIn`/`updateSupplierIn`, `LocationsAdmin.createLocationIn`/`updateLocationIn`, `PriceListAdmin.replaceEntriesIn`, refactored out of their handlers), so every row is audited and published as by hand; codes (category, species, tax category, branch, price list, grade, band) are looked up among live records only; two rows naming one record are refused (`Duplicate of row N`). **Products:** named by `sku`; columns `sku`, `name`, `category`, `stockPolicy`, `species`, `baseUom`, `saleUom`, `taxCategory`, `nominalPackKg`, `shelfLifeDays`, `barcode`, `description`, `onlineMode`; a new SKU needs name, category, policy and both units, and is created ACTIVE (as `createProduct`, A-083) and `HIDDEN` online unless stated; the policy, species and units of an existing product never change by import; no SEO, status, withdrawal, storage or tolerance column (set in the form). **Suppliers:** named by `supplierNo`; columns `supplierNo`, `name`, `contactName`, `phone`, `email`, `address`, `categories` (codes separated by `;`, replacing the list when stated); no TIN column, as for customers; no status column. **Locations:** named by `branch` and `code`; physical kinds only, virtual locations never imported or changed; kind and temperature class never change; a parent is a live location of the same branch or a row above that creates it; no `allowsNegative` column (an overdraw permission is set in the form only); applying checks the location permission at each branch the file writes (ADR-0017), so a grant at one branch never writes another's; the export leaves out virtual locations. **Price list entries:** money in integer pesewas (`listPricePesewas` and so on, `85000` is GHS 850.00), never cedis with a decimal point, so the file says the same number the contract says and nothing is converted; rows go into the named list's one `DRAFT` version and never an active or submitted one: a list with no draft or with a version waiting for approval is a row error, and the import never drafts a version (the manager drafts, imports, then submits for maker-checker approval, FR-MDM-015); a row matching a draft entry on product, grade, band and minimum quantity replaces it, any other row adds one, and entries the file does not name stay; the band rules (floor <= list <= ceiling, a priced entry's floor above zero) are row errors; applying checks `mdm.price_list.update` at each version's branch; the export is each list's version active now, drafts left out. **Opening stock** stays 422 `IMPORT_ENTITY_NOT_AVAILABLE` until B5 (A-128) | Owner | B04e continued 5 | Open; its product part superseded by A-250 (B09q, 2026-11-17: SKUs are the server's, no barcode column) |

| A-134 | 2026-11-15 | How the ledger triggers read ADR-0002 and ADR-0018 where they are silent, each the most conservative reading. (a) The value of every movement leaving a physical location is set by the database from the cost pool; a caller's figure is ignored. (b) A reversal carries its original's value exactly and is refused (`ck_stock_reversal_value`) when the pool can no longer give that value up, for example a receipt reversed after part of it was issued at average; the correction is then an adjustment. A reversal of a reversal is refused. (c) A serial unit has no location until its first movement, which must come from a virtual location; its status follows the virtual kind it moves to (`CUSTOMER` sold, `PROCESSING` consumed, `LOSS` written off, any other depleted) and is `ACTIVE` at a physical one. (d) A lot is `DEPLETED` when its physical on-hand reaches zero and `ACTIVE` again if it rises. (e) A movement's branch is its physical source's branch, or its destination's for a receipt. (f) A movement that names its reservation consumes it whole. (g) An allowed-negative movement that dips below reserved raises a `RESERVATION_CONFLICT` exception instead of failing, as its negative stock does. (h) A hold blocks when it covers the movement's `occurred_at` and is unreleased. (i) `FEED_ISSUE` and `MEDICATION_ISSUE` publish no ledger event of their own (the `CostCapitalised` or `UpkeepExpensed` that follows carries the value, B6), nor does `BIRTH` (nil cost); the others publish the event of the ADR-0003 matrix. (j) Refusing the sale of an expired lot (FR-INV-016) is not yet in the trigger; it lands with the expiry alerts in B05 continued | Owner, accountant for (b) and (i) | B05 continued | Open; (j) built in B05 continued 4 (migration 0064, `ck_stock_expired`) |

(f) A receipt is not dated more than five minutes after the server's clock. (g) The receiving location is a live physical location other than in-transit and a site. (h) A tag or batch already ACTIVE for the product is refused at posting (`TAG_IN_USE`, `BATCH_IN_USE`). (i) `receiptNo` stays null until document numbering exists (B8, A-122). (a) `priceBand` on a stock line is null: which channel's band a stock screen shows is undecided, and cost and price are never mixed (CR-001). | A-139 | 2026-11-15 | How movements, transfers, adjustments and reversals read FR-INV-004 and FR-INV-010 to FR-INV-012 where they are silent, each the most conservative reading (migrations 0050 to 0053). **Who:** the Owner, Manager and Storekeeper read movements, transfers and adjustments, reverse a movement, draft, dispatch and receive transfers, and draft and submit adjustments; the Owner and Manager hold `inv.adjustment.approve`, the checker of both `STOCK_WRITE_OFF` and `STOCK_REVERSAL` (the contract names no other approver permission); the Accountant reads all three and writes none; every other role holds none. **Policies:** both kinds need approval at or above 50,000 pesewas (FR-INV-012's seed) with a fresh step-up, as every other approval policy asks. The threshold is read through IAM's new `Approvals.judgeIn` and copied onto the document when it is judged, so the database checks the posted row against its own threshold (`ck_stock_adjustment_approved`, `ck_stock_reversal_approved`) instead of reading `iam.approval_policy` from an `inv` trigger as `docs/data-model/inv.md` first said (that would widen ADR-0047). **Adjustments:** a reason from the `STOCK_ADJUSTMENT` or `WASTAGE` lists (the contract's own wording), a note when the reason requires one; a loss is valued at what its pool would give up and a gain at the pool's average (`valueAtAverage`, under the 100 percent gate), never at a figure from the request; the total compared with the threshold is the sum of the lines' values; animals are never adjusted either way (they leave by sale, death or cull with their own controls, B6); a typed weight is recorded `MANUAL`, since the contract's line carries no source; a gain with value is tagged cost element `PURCHASE`; the approval binds to the lines and their values as the pools stand, so a pool that moves before the decision voids it; the posting movements' actor is the checker. **Transfers:** both ends are live physical locations other than in-transit and a site; dispatch goes to the source branch's in-transit location (a branch without one is refused; the seed adds Dodowa's `TRANSIT`); any hold blocks dispatch, inside a branch too, because `TRANSFER_DISPATCH` is `blocked_by_hold` in migration 0044; an animal must be at the source when drafted and moves whole; a dispatched transfer is never cancelled; a shortfall needs a reason from the stock adjustment or wastage lists, is posted from in-transit to the branch's LOSS location as `ADJUST_LOSS`, and raises a `TRANSFER_VARIANCE` stock exception; receiving more than was dispatched is refused. FR-INV-011's approval of a large variance is not built: `receiveStockTransfer` has no 202 in the contract, and adding one is a contract change for the owner; the exception queue is the control until then. **Reversals:** each is an `inv.stock_reversal` document (new document type `STOCK_REVERSAL`, not numbered), the reversing movement's source document; the reason is from the `STOCK_ADJUSTMENT` list and the note is required; a transfer's movements, value-only movements and reversals are never reversed by hand; a movement whose pool can no longer give its value back is refused (`STOCK_REVERSAL_NOT_POSSIBLE`, `POOL_VALUE_GONE`), the correction then being an adjustment. **Reads:** a movement is visible where its branch and either location is reached by a grant; a single movement is scoped to the physical location it left (or reached, for a receipt); a transfer is readable from either end. `transferNo` and `adjustmentNo` stay null until numbering (B8). New error codes: `STOCK_TRANSFER_INVALID`, `TRANSFER_VARIANCE`, `STOCK_ADJUSTMENT_INVALID`, `STOCK_REVERSAL_NOT_POSSIBLE`. The IAM approval tests that borrowed `STOCK_WRITE_OFF` and `inv.adjustment.approve` as a probe now use `SALE_VOID` and a test-only `iam.probe.approve` | Owner | B05 continued 3 | Resolved by the owner on 2026-11-15: the 50,000 pesewa threshold with step-up and never adjusting a live animal are confirmed; copying the threshold onto each document is confirmed, also so that a later policy change cannot rewrite an approval's history; no approval at receipt, but the shortfall is held and written off only with approval under the adjustment rule, through `resolveStockException` (ADR-0048, built in B05 continued 3b) |

| A-141 | 2026-11-15 | How stock takes and cycle counts read FR-INV-013 and FR-INV-014 where they are silent, each the most conservative reading (migrations 0054 to 0056). **Who:** the Owner, Manager and Storekeeper read, plan, start, cancel and count (FR-INV-013's counter); only the Owner and Manager submit and post (its reviewer, `inv.stock_take.post`); a variance value at or above the `STOCK_WRITE_OFF` threshold (seed 50,000 pesewas, the existing policy) waits for a different person holding `inv.adjustment.approve` with a fresh step-up; the Accountant reads; every other role holds none. **Scope:** one live physical location other than in-transit and a site; `FULL` lists no products, `CYCLE` lists at least one, each once; the contract carries `productIds` only, so a cycle count by category is not offered; animals are never counted here (`ANIMAL_NOT_COUNTED`, and a full take leaves them out), because a missing animal is a death, a cull or a theft with its own controls (B6), never a stock-take loss. **Overlap:** a take holds its scope from `DRAFT` to `PENDING_APPROVAL`; a new take whose location matches and whose products meet (a full take meets every product) is refused (`SCOPE_OVERLAP`) at planning and at start, and the database refuses a start that overlaps a take counting, in review or pending (`ck_stock_take_overlap`, under an advisory lock on the location, so at any isolation level); counts of the same location only, not of its child locations. **Expected:** every non-zero balance in scope at start, lots and loose stock, with a listed loose product counted from zero when nothing is on hand; hidden from every reader, not only the counter, while `DRAFT` or `COUNTING`. **Recount:** the tolerance is zero, so every line whose first count differs from the expected figure is recounted once in review before the take can be submitted, and the recount is final; the contract's example shows a difference without a recount, which a tolerance setting would allow later. **Values:** as adjustments (A-139): a loss at what the pool would give up, a gain at its average, valued at review and again at submit; the threshold compares gains and losses together; the approval binds to the lines and their values, so a pool that moves voids it; a refused or withdrawn approval cancels the take (`The variances were not approved`), and a new take counts again. **Movements:** a loss posts `STOCKTAKE_LOSS` to the branch's LOSS location with reason `COUNT_ERROR`, a gain `STOCKTAKE_GAIN` from its ADJUSTMENT location with reason `FOUND_STOCK` (both lists' fixed codes) and cost element `PURCHASE` when it carries value; no weight is posted, since the expected weight is not frozen; a typed counted weight is kept as `MANUAL`; the checker is the actor of the movements. **Cancel:** from `DRAFT`, `COUNTING` or `REVIEW` with a reason; a pending take is decided by its approval. `stockTakeNo` stays null until numbering (B8). New error code `STOCK_TAKE_INVALID`. IAM keeps one handler per approval kind, so INV's `StockWriteOffs` hands a `STOCK_WRITE_OFF` to the adjustment or the stock take by the subject's document type | Owner | B05 continued 4 | Open |

| A-144 | 2026-11-15 | How reservations, exceptions, availability and the stock unit list read FR-INV-005, FR-INV-021, FR-INV-023 and FR-INV-025 where they are silent, each the most conservative reading (migrations 0060 to 0062). **Who:** the Owner, Manager, Accountant and Storekeeper read reservations; only the Owner and Manager release one by hand, with a reason; the Owner, Manager and Accountant read exceptions and only the Owner and Manager resolve them; availability and the stock unit list read under `inv.stock.read`; every other role holds none. **Reserving** is in process only (`Reservations.reserveIn`, `consumeIn`, `releaseIn`), by the module that holds the checkout, order or quote, in its serializable transaction, naming `reservationLockKey` in `seriesLocks` so concurrent reservations of one balance queue (A-060); never at a virtual location, in transit or at a site, never of a unit under a hold in force (`ck_stock_reservation_place`, `ck_stock_reservation_held`, migration 0060), never above available, one active per animal; the holder sets the expiry (checkout 15 minutes, A-045; null until fulfilment). A consumed reservation is consumed whole (A-134 (f)). **Expiry:** `reservation-expiry` every minute as the Scheduler, up to 500 per run, rows another run holds skipped; each end publishes `inv.ReservationExpired` (or `inv.ReservationReleased` by hand) through the outbox; the released reason now takes 500 characters, as the contract's `ReasonRequest`. **Exceptions:** listed newest first, scoped to their branch and location; a resolution kind is required: `WRITE_OFF` or `FOUND` for a held transfer variance only, `ADJUSTED` (a posted adjustment naming the exception) or `NO_CORRECTION` for every other; with no kind given, a document means `ADJUSTED` and none `NO_CORRECTION`, so nothing a client sends was made required; an adjustment by hand never names a held variance and never adjusts in transit. **Availability:** per product over the branch's physical stock locations or the one named, in transit and sites left out; a unit under a hold in force counts all its on hand as held (A-137 (d)); `LOW_STOCK` at five or fewer (SA-19). **Stock units:** `q` is part of the tag or batch without regard to case; scope as holds (A-142). | A-145 | 2026-11-15 | How ADR-0048 is built, where it leaves the choice to the phase. (a) **The held state** adds no movement type, location kind or exception kind: the shortfall stays in the source branch's `IN_TRANSIT` location, still the farm's stock at its value in the pool, held by its open `TRANSFER_VARIANCE` exception, which has no movement; the hand count is unchanged (in-transit stock is counted). The database refuses any movement that would take an in-transit balance below what open variances hold (`ck_stock_held`) and any sale, processing or dispatch out of transit (`ck_stock_in_transit`). (b) **The threshold** is the adjustment rule exactly, as the ADR says: approval at or above 50,000 pesewas (`Approvals.judgeIn`, as FR-INV-012), although the ADR's prose says above; at 50,000 itself it therefore waits, the stricter reading. (c) **The write-off** is a stock adjustment in transit with the reason the receiver recorded and the resolver's note, valued from the pool when submitted, never at the value noted at receipt (`heldValuePesewas` in the exception's details is for information); the exception is resolved when the loss posts, by whoever lets it post (the checker above the threshold). An animal is never written off this way (animals are never adjusted, A-139); a missing animal is found or waits for its own controls (B6). (d) **Found** needs no approval (no expense): a stock location in either branch of the transfer, posted as a `TRANSFER_RECEIPT` against the transfer, which is the resolution document. (e) While a write-off waits, nothing else is decided; a rejected one leaves the variance held. (f) **B8:** no event is published at receipt; the ledger's holding account reads the open variances and posts from the write-off's `ADJUST_LOSS` event or the found `TRANSFER_RECEIPT`; whether a held variance needs its own event is B8's to decide | Owner, accountant for (f) | B8 | Open |

| A-147 | 2026-11-15 | How the cold chain, reorder rules and stock alerts read FR-INV-007, FR-INV-008, FR-INV-015 and FR-INV-016 where they are silent, each the most conservative reading (migrations 0063 to 0066). **Who:** the Owner, the Manager and the Storekeeper manage reorder rules (FR-INV-015's actors) and record temperature readings (FR-INV-007's); the Accountant reads both; every other role, the Driver included (the contract's example shows a van reading by a driver), holds none until the owner says otherwise; alerts read under `inv.stock.read`. **Readings:** only at a live physical location that has a permitted range in master data (a cold room or a vehicle; any other is `NO_TEMPERATURE_RANGE`); the range is read through MDM's `MasterDataRefs` and copied onto the reading, so the database checks the excursion flag against it without reading `mdm` (ADR-0047 not widened); both bounds are in range; a time more than five minutes ahead of the server is refused; the client's id makes a resubmission return the first reading. **Excursion:** in the reading's transaction, one `TEMPERATURE_EXCURSION` document; a `QUARANTINE` hold placed through `StockHolds.placeIn` on every active lot and animal with stock in the location, from the reading's time (so a later-synced sale for a time after it is refused too), open-ended, placed by the person who recorded the reading (so that person never releases it, A-142); one `TEMPERATURE_EXCURSION` exception per stock line there, untracked stock included (it cannot be held, and its exception says so), resolved as any other exception (`ADJUSTED` or `NO_CORRECTION`); the reading's `exceptionId` is the first; an excursion in an empty room raises no exception; a hold is placed for every excursion, even on a lot already quarantined, so releasing one never releases another; `inv.TemperatureExcursionRecorded` through the outbox. **Rules:** one live rule per product and branch, or per product and one physical stock location of it (not in transit, not a site); minimum at or under the reorder point at or under a maximum above zero; lead time 1 to 365 days. **Available** is on hand less reserved less held over the rule's scope, in transit and sites left out (as availability, A-144). **Level alerts:** `LOW_STOCK` at or below the reorder point, `BELOW_MINIMUM` strictly under the minimum, each raised once and cleared once stock rises above its level (the minimum: at it); judged in the transaction of every `Stock.postIn`, reservation made or ended and hold placed or released, and again by the daily job, which catches what no transaction sees (a hold ending by time); the suggestion is the maximum less what is available. `StockAlert` gains `suggestedQty` (an additive response property). **Expiry alerts:** by the daily `stock-alerts` job (06:30 Africa/Accra, docs/OPERATIONS.md), per lot and location with stock: `EXPIRING` within the lead time of the product's rule at that location, else its branch-wide rule, else the default of 3 days (FR-INV-016's seed, a named constant until a settings table exists), `EXPIRED` after the expiry date in Africa/Accra; an alert clears when the lot has nothing left there, and an expiring alert when the expired one is raised. **Expired lots:** a sale and an offline sale of a lot after its expiry date are refused by the database (`blocked_when_expired`, `STOCK_UNIT_EXPIRED`); processing, transfer and write-off are not, so an expired lot can still be written off; an offline sale of one is refused like a sale under hold and becomes B9b's conflict to record (ADR-0027). **Jobs:** the nightly recompute runs at 02:00 as `stock-balance-recompute` and records `SUCCESS` with its drift count and exception ids in its summary, since `ScheduledJob` records `SUCCESS` or `FAILED` only (ADR-0049, not widened); a drift is still raised as `BALANCE_DRIFT` exceptions, an event and an error log. New error codes `REORDER_RULE_INVALID`, `TEMPERATURE_READING_INVALID`, `STOCK_UNIT_EXPIRED` | Owner | B05x (the low stock tile), B9b (offline conflicts) | Open |

A goods receipt of kind `OPENING` stays refused over HTTP (`OPENING_NOT_AVAILABLE`): A-136 (a) is decided that way, not lifted. **Rows:** `branch`, `location`, `sku`, `qty`, `valuePesewas` required; one row per untracked product and location, per lot (`batch`, with `expiresOn` when the product has a shelf life), or per animal (`tag`, `sex`, `qty` 1); `weightKg` is recorded `MANUAL`; the value is the line's cost in integer pesewas and must be above zero (an opening balance with no value would understate stock), so an animal's own value is kept per row rather than split across a line. **Once:** a product that has any balance in the branch already (stock history) is a row error, so opening stock is loaded before trading and a second load of the same file is refused; a batch or tag in use, or repeated in the file, is a row error. **Posting:** one goods receipt of kind `OPENING` per location, drafted and posted through `GoodsReceipts` in the import's transaction, received now (never backdated), from the branch's virtual OPENING location as `OPENING_BALANCE` movements through `Stock.postIn`, tagged cost element `PURCHASE` (the closest of the six; FIN may map it to opening balance equity in B8), each publishing `inv.OpeningStockLoaded`. **Export:** the empty template (the header row), since stock on hand is reported by `listStockOnHand` and never re-imported | Owner, accountant for the cost element | B8 (the posting of `inv.OpeningStockLoaded`) | Open |

| A-153 | 2026-11-15 | How a processing order runs (FR-PRC-001 to FR-PRC-008, FR-PRC-011, B6), each the most conservative reading where the requirements, the data model and the planned contract differ. **Statuses:** `PLANNED`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED`, as the data model and contract name them (the SRS says DRAFT for PLANNED). **Nothing moves until completion:** the planned contract said starting posts the PROCESS_CONSUME movements, but FR-PRC-003 consumes at completion and FR-PRC-011 cancels an order in progress with no stock effect, so the inputs are checked at create, start and completion and consumed only at completion; the start description is corrected. **Inputs:** live animals only (SERIAL stock units), standing at the processing location, which is any physical location of the branch that holds stock (the pens on the seeded farm); a carcass lot as an input is refused (`INPUT_NOT_AN_ANIMAL`) until a later session needs it (BL-051). | A-158 | 2026-11-15 | How B06 continued reads LIV where the requirements, the data model and the planned contract are silent or differ, each the most conservative reading. **Split:** B06 continued split at a LIV sub-domain boundary (session-openers "If a session cannot finish"): this session built the register, weights, health events with withdrawal holds and veterinary cost, mortality and culls, rations and feed (17 operations), whose rules carry the gate; animal edits and moves, the timeline, growth, batches and breeding and births (15 operations) move to **B06 continued 2**, and PRC's packs, labels, the reversing order of FR-PRC-011 and the Farm hand's PRC access (A-153) to **B06 continued 3**, both inserted before B7. **Register:** an animal is opened in the receipt's transaction by a transactional subscriber to `inv.StockUnitRegistered` (purchase when the receipt names a supplier, opening stock otherwise), at the pool value the receipt carried, with the receipt's weight as its first weight record when one was given with its source; a second live animal with a tag already live is refused with `TAG_IN_USE`, which also refuses the receipt. Its status follows `inv.StockIssuedForSale`, `inv.StockConsumedInProcessing`, `inv.StockWrittenOff`, `inv.SaleReturnReceived` and `inv.SupplierReturnIssued` in their own transactions: SOLD (a sale or a return to the supplier), SLAUGHTERED, DEAD (a death, and any other write-off, for which the register has no other word) or CULLED, and ALIVE again on a customer return or the reversal of the closing movement. Once closed it shows the cost its closing movement carried out (`exit_value_pesewas`, an addition) and no location. `priceBand` and `weightBand` are what the pricing engine resolves for it at the counter of its branch now, by its last weight and grade, or null when the engine cannot. `sellable` and `weightBandId` are filtered in chunks after the database query because inventory and pricing answer them. **Weights:** the latest weighing is copied onto the stock unit's last weight through INV (`StockUnits.recordWeightIn`), so its label prints it; an older one changes nothing there. A flock's weighing is the average of a sample no larger than its head. **Health:** medication is an untracked product of no species, issued from the branch's STORE location by MEDICATION_ISSUE; `costPesewas` in the request is a veterinary service charged beside it, and the event's cost is the medication's value plus that charge, spread by head-days (`liv.health_cost_allocation`, an addition with its own deferred check); `liv.HealthEventRecorded` names the two parts so B8 can credit the service charge elsewhere than 1300. The withdrawal hold ends exactly the withdrawal days after `occurredAt`, to the minute (so the 28 days after 1 November end on 29 November at the same time), and a request may lengthen the product's withdrawal days, never shorten them. A pen-wide event treats every live animal and flock standing there now, one hold each. **Head-days:** a feed issue or a treatment is for the day it is recorded on, so an animal weighs one head-day and a flock its whole head on hand; the leftover pesewas go to the lowest tag or batch number. A flock is GROWING (capitalised) until batches give it a purpose (B06 continued 2); a breeding animal's share is expensed and published as `inv.UpkeepExpensed` through a new `Stock.expenseIn`, with no movement. Feed comes from the branch's STORE unless the record names another physical location of the branch, and is posted when recorded (never DRAFT). **Mortality:** its own approval kind `MORTALITY_WRITE_OFF` (threshold 50,000, approver `liv.mortality.approve`, which requires step-up, as `inv.adjustment.approve` does), not the contract's `STOCK_WRITE_OFF`, so the livestock handler decides it and a stock adjustment approver is not by that alone a mortality approver; the contract gains the enum value (1.24.0). The photograph must be an EVIDENCE file the scanner has passed (CLEAN), checked by the application and by the database (`platform.require_clean_file`); it is given at report, since no operation changes a record. Only the reporter submits, so the maker IAM keeps from checking is the reporter. A record waiting for approval blocks a second report of the animal (`ALREADY_AWAITING_APPROVAL`); a REPORTED one does not, so a report refused for want of a photograph can be made again with one. A flock's write-off is valued at the pool's average for its head (INV's `issue`). Every submission publishes `liv.MortalityReported` with the reporter, the location, the value and the threshold, for the daily exception report's mortality rate by handler (B12, abuse case 2). **Permissions:** the Owner and Manager hold every LIV permission; the Storekeeper reads and records but neither manages rations nor approves; the Accountant reads only; the Vet or Farm hand records weights, health events, feed and mortality and reads weights, and is granted no read whose shape carries a cost they did not enter (the register's accumulated cost, the health cost, the feed spread, the value written off: DC-11), as A-153 decided for processing, until the owner decides otherwise. **Seed:** the seed weighs the six animals and a sample of the broilers; feed, medicine, a store and rations are seeded with batches in B06 continued 2, so that the B5 and B05x hand counts are counted again once | Owner, accountant for the health cost parts and the expensed upkeep | B06 continued | Owner, 2026-11-15: the split is approved; MORTALITY_WRITE_OFF is approved as its own approval kind (the photo rule belongs to deaths, not to every write-off); the Farm hand line is **not** approved as written: the role reads the animal register, feed rations and health events with withdrawal holds (food safety), in cost-free shapes, never a cost; death records and money-only reads stay refused. B06 continued 3 builds it first, and closes the cost B06 continued 2's grants exposed (migration 0082). **Built 2026-11-15 (B06 continued 3, A-160, ADR-0052), the owner's decision as a matrix for the Vet or Farm hand:** reads without cost: the register (`listCareAnimals`, `getCareAnimal`), health events with withdrawal holds (`listCareHealthEvents`, `getCareHealthEvent`), rations (`listCareRations`), batches (`listCareBatches`, `getCareBatch`), weights (`listWeightRecords`), growth (`listGrowth`) and breeding (`listBreedingRecords`, `getBreedingRecord`); writes answered without cost: weights, health events, feed, mortality report and submit, animal edits and moves, batch hatch, purpose and close (each through its care twin), and breeding and births; refused: death records, the feed consumption reads, the timeline, the full register, health and batch reads, every full write whose answer carries cost, rations management, mortality approval, and every PRC operation; **owner, 2026-11-15 (B06r, the frontend's F6), the role matrix changes for the Vet or Farm hand:** it is the maker of a death report: it finds the animal by its exact tag (`getCareAnimalByTag`, `liv.care.read`), reports, uploads the photograph and submits; it may upload `EVIDENCE` files and nothing else (`platform.file.upload.evidence`, new, ADR-0053); it never writes stock off alone, so below the threshold its submission waits for a confirmation by the Owner or the Manager (`liv.mortality.confirm`, new) and at or above it for `liv.mortality.approve` with step-up as before; other reporters keep the direct write-off below the threshold (`liv.mortality.post`, new: Owner, Manager, Storekeeper). Death records and money-only reads stay refused (A-175) |
| A-159 | 2026-11-15 | How B06 continued 2 reads LIV where the requirements, the data model and the planned contract are silent or differ, each the most conservative reading. **Edits:** breed, sex, purpose and grade change; species, product, branch, acquisition date, source, supplier, cost, parentage and the tag never change (the database's `liv.animal_guard`); the tag is the stock unit's identifier, which inventory holds fixed, so `updateAnimal` accepts `tagNo` only unchanged (`TAG_NOT_EDITABLE`; correcting a misread tag is BL-056); a closed animal is not edited; the sex of a dam with a breeding record is fixed (FR-LIV-002, also in the database). The grade is the unit's, set through INV's new `StockUnits.setGradeIn`. **Moves:** the destination is a live physical location of the animal's branch that is not the SITE or IN_TRANSIT; the move starts where inventory says the animal stands (the trigger insists too), is authorised at both pens, and is recorded in `liv.animal_move` (an addition, a LEDGER table, with document type `ANIMAL_MOVE`). The TRANSFER_INTERNAL movement carries the animal's cost from pen to pen, as the trigger values every movement; the pool is unchanged. **Growth:** a pen's members are the animals and flocks inventory had standing there both at the window's first instant and its last (`StockUnits.onHandAsOfIn`, new), so an animal moved in or out during the window is left out; a batch's member is its flock's lot, with head at both ends. One row per pen and species. Averages are per head, each member weighted by its head, over members weighed on two different days in the window; average daily gain is the weighed head's total gain over their total days between first and last weighing, to three places; a loss of weight shows null gain, daily gain and conversion, since the contract's kilograms are never negative. Feed is the posted consumptions with `consumedOn` in the window: a pen takes its species' head-day share of each issue to it (the whole issue when one species is fed), a batch its head-day share, summed exactly and rounded once. Feed conversion is stated to **three** places as FR-LIV-016 says, not the contract's former "two decimals" (its description and examples are corrected in 1.25.0). Mortality is posted deaths, not culls, found in the window over the head at its start; a batch's is all its posted deaths over its initial head. Africa/Accra keeps GMT, so business days are UTC days. **Batches:** a flock received as a lot of a livestock product opens its batch in the receipt's transaction (numbered as the lot, GROWING, started the day it arrived, refused with `BATCH_NO_TAKEN` if the number is taken). `createBatch` hatches a flock: it registers a lot of the species' one lot-tracked product and brings its head into the pen by a BIRTH movement at nil cost (document type `LIVESTOCK_BATCH`); a species with no such product, or more than one, is refused (`NO_FLOCK_PRODUCT`), so batches that only group individual animals are not built (BL-055; `liv.animal.batch_id` is added by the expand and stays empty until they are). A batch's purpose decides its flock's cost treatment: GROWING capitalised, BREEDING and LAYING expensed (a laying flock is kept to produce, not sold). A batch closes only when no head is left; the reason is always required, and with head on hand the close is refused whatever the reason (`BATCH_NOT_EMPTY`), since closing would hide stock still there. A closed batch is frozen. **Breeding:** gestation days are data in a LIV lookup, `liv.gestation_period`, keyed by species code (goat 150, sheep 147, pig 114, rabbit 31, SA-19), and a species with a row is a breeding species; the mating day is day one of gestation, as FR-LIV-010's own example (1 March 2027 to 28 July 2027) requires. The dam is a live female, the sire a male of the same species; a mating is not in the future; a confirmed mating is never set back to MATED. A birth registers each live offspring through INV's new `StockUnits.registerSerialIn` and a BIRTH movement of one head at nil cost from the branch's PRODUCTION location into the pen where the dam stands, opens its record with source BIRTH, its dam, sire and birth, the dam's breed and purpose GROWING, links its lineage to the dam (`BIRTH`), and records the litter, live births and stillbirths (an addition). The dam must have been female and alive on the birth date: a dam closed on a day before the birth is refused (`DAM_NOT_ALIVE_ON_BIRTH`); a dam standing in no pen now is refused too (`DAM_NOT_IN_A_PEN`), since the offspring would have nowhere to be. **Permissions:** the Owner and Manager hold every new permission; the Storekeeper edits and moves animals, reads and starts batches, and reads and records breeding; the Accountant reads growth, batches and breeding. The Vet or Farm hand, the requirements' actor, edits and moves animals, reads, starts and closes batches, reads growth and reads and records breeding: the owner decided on 2026-11-15 that the Farm hand is not refused a read needed for animal work because it carries cost, and B06 continued 3 builds the cost-free shapes. Until then these answer the Farm hand with cost: `updateAnimal` and `moveAnimal` (the animal's `accumulatedCostPesewas`), `listBatches`, `getBatch`, `createBatch`, `updateBatch`, `closeBatch` (the batch's `accumulatedCostPesewas`). The timeline is read with `liv.animal.read`, which the Farm hand does not yet hold (A-158's matrix, unchanged here). **Seed:** the Dodowa STORE, a feed and medicine category, broiler grower, goat and sheep concentrate and albendazole, their first delivery (GHS 1,940.00) and three rations, each through its own module's service; the B5 hand count becomes GHS 17,895.01 and the B05x purchases on the seed day GHS 17,290.01 over five receipts | Owner; accountant for the laying flock's treatment and the nil-cost hatch; vet for the gestation count | B06 continued 2 | Open |
| A-160 | 2026-11-15 | How B06 continued 3 builds the owner's Farm hand decision (A-158 as decided), each the most conservative reading. **Pattern:** the codebase had no cost-visibility pattern for staff, so the role gets cost-free operations of its own under `/care/` with their own shapes, as ADR-0029 gives customers and investors theirs (ADR-0052); never nullable or omitted cost fields. **Shapes:** the full shape with every cost and **price** field removed (`accumulatedCostPesewas`, `priceBand`, `costPesewas`, `valuePesewas`, `amountPesewas`, and `currency` with them), since a selling price band is a pesewa figure the owner did not open to the role either. **Reads:** `liv.care.read` (new) opens the register, the health events with their withdrawal days, end and holds, the rations and the batches; the role gives up `liv.batch.read`. **Writes:** the role keeps every write A-158 and A-159 gave it, through care twins answered without cost; a full write whose answer carries cost needs that answer's read permission as well (`x-answer-permission`), which refuses only the Farm hand, so the walk shows no full answer reaches it. This also closes the cost in the answers of `recordHealthEvent`, `recordFeedConsumption`, `reportMortality` and `submitMortalityRecord`, which A-158 granted and which leaked too, not only migration 0082's. **Not opened:** death records (`listMortalityRecords`, `getMortalityRecord`), the feed consumption reads (their value), the timeline (each movement's value), rations management, and all of PRC (A-153: every order answers its costs; packs and labels are the Storekeeper's cold-room work, FR-PRC-009 and FR-PRC-010). **The matrix as built** is recorded in A-158's resolution. | Owner | B06 continued 3 | Open |
| A-161 | 2026-11-15 | How B06 continued 3 reads packs and labels (FR-PRC-009, FR-PRC-010), each the most conservative reading. **Packing:** a saleable output of a COMPLETED, unreversed order is packed once, in one call, while its lot is whole where the order put it; the pack weights sum **exactly** to the output's weight (FR-PRC-009's 0.000 kg, not the planned contract's 0.050 kg; the last pack is the remainder); when the output recorded a `packCount`, there are that many packs. Packing moves no stock and no cost. A pack weighs under 1,000 kg, the most six digits of grams carry. The database checks the sum at commit (`ck_pack_weights`) and freezes a pack's weight, lot and barcode. **Barcode:** CODE_128 (A-151), payload `<lot>/<grams, six digits>` exactly as FR-PRC-010's example; it carries nothing else, so a reprint gives the same payload, and two packs of one lot that weigh the same carry the same payload: they are interchangeable, so `prc.pack.barcode` is **not unique** (a deviation from `docs/data-model/prc.md`, which said UNIQUE; FR-PRC-010's own lot packs as 15.000, 15.000 and 14.600). The counter's "second scan of the same pack is refused" is B9b's, by pack status. **Labels:** `printPackLabels` answers the label data for the client to draw, as `getStockUnitLabel` does (A-151: no image rendered), not the planned `SignedUrl` to a PDF; it prints only packs IN_STOCK or PICKED, records the first print time once and marks a reprint; it takes an Idempotency-Key, since it records the print. **Seed:** no pack is seeded, because packs need a completed order and the seed processes none (its completion would change the stock the B5 and B05x gates count by hand, B6's reason); the tests process their own. **Permissions:** as the order's: `prc.order.read` lists, `prc.order.process` packs and prints. | Owner | B06 continued 3; B9b (scanning a pack at the counter) | Open |
| A-162 | 2026-11-15 | How B06 continued 3 reads the reversing order (FR-PRC-011), each the most conservative reading. **Shape:** the opener's `cancelProcessingOrder` on a COMPLETED order; the order stays COMPLETED and frozen (FR-PRC-011: "cannot be edited") and answers its `reversal`; the reversal is its own document, `prc.processing_reversal` (document type `PROCESSING_REVERSAL`), the source document of the reversing movements, once per order. The data model's `reverses_order_id` (a second order row) is left unused: an order with no inputs or outputs would fail the order's own sums and appear in yield. **Unsold:** every saleable output's lot is whole where the order put it (none sold, moved or adjusted), not reserved, and none of its packs picked, sold or written off; otherwise 422 `OUTPUT_SOLD` (`OUTPUT_RESERVED`), and nothing moves. **Movements:** each PROCESS_OUTPUT, then each PROCESS_CONSUME, is reversed through INV's new `StockMovements.reverseForDocumentIn`, the exact opposite carrying the original's value (ADR-0018), with no reason code and no STOCK_REVERSAL approval, since the order's reversal is its own authorised command; the animal is ACTIVE in its pen again at its prior pool value and LIV's register, which follows the reversal of the closing movement, shows it ALIVE. | A-163 | 2026-11-15 | How B06u builds FR-MDM-003's alternative units, taking the most conservative reading where the documents leave a choice. **(a) The factor:** one alternative unit is `numerator / denominator` base units, two `bigint` columns and never a stored decimal (docs/data-model/mdm.md); "exact decimals to three places" (FR-MDM-003) is read as: in lowest terms the denominator divides 1,000, so a 50 kg bag is 50/1, a 500 g pack 1/2, and a third (1/3, or 0.3333333 as 3333333/10000000) is refused. The server reduces a fraction it is sent (250/2000 is stored as 1/8); the database refuses one not in lowest terms or not exact (`ck_uom_conversion_exact`, migration 0090), a numerator above a billion (`ck_uom_conversion_numerator`), and a converted quantity that would need a fourth decimal place is refused, never rounded (`INEXACT_QUANTITY`). **(b) Which products convert:** only a `COUNTABLE` product, from a counted unit (`COUNT` kind) other than its base unit, to its base unit; a catch-weight product never (its weighed kilograms are charged, A-157), nor a serialised animal (counted by the head) nor a service. FR-MDM-003's "15 kg pack at factor 15.000" is therefore proved as arithmetic in the unit test, not as a conversion any meat product may hold. Held in the database too (`mdm.uom_conversion_guard`, HK010). **(c) Never edited:** a conversion's product, units and factor never change (HK004); a new factor is a new conversion after the old one is archived, so a quantity converted earlier keeps its factor. So the table has an `id` key and a partial unique index among live rows (`uq_uom_conversion_live`), not the data model's primary key (product, from, to), which would forbid the archived row beside its replacement. **(d) Operations:** `listUomConversions` (CR-011's name; `mdm.product.read`, filters `productId`, `includeArchived`), `createUomConversion` (`mdm.uom_conversion.create`) and `archiveUomConversion` (`mdm.uom_conversion.archive`), both new permissions granted to the Owner and the Manager (FR-MDM-003's actor), as the rest of the catalogue is (0020). No conversion appears on `Uom` or `Product`: additive, a separate list. Each change publishes `mdm.ProductChanged` with `uomConversions` as its changed field, as the gallery does with `images`. **(e) Other modules** convert only through `MasterDataRefs.toBaseIn` (base unit passes through; `NO_PRODUCT`, `NO_CONVERSION`, `INEXACT_QUANTITY`). **(f) Seed:** the broiler grower pellets in 50 kg bags, the goat and sheep concentrate in 25 kg bags, cabbages in crates of 24. | Owner | B7 (the first purchase line in another unit) | Open |
| A-164 | 2026-11-15 | How B7 builds purchase to pay (FR-PUR-005 to FR-PUR-010, FR-PUR-012), taking the most conservative reading where the documents leave a choice. **(a) The approval limit:** one `PURCHASE_ORDER` approval policy for every branch, judged by IAM's `Approvals.judgeIn` ("at or above"), seeded at 200,001 pesewas so that "above 200,000" (FR-PUR-005) holds exactly; a requester's own `iam.role_limit` of kind `PURCHASE_ORDER_APPROVAL` is not read yet. The threshold judged is copied onto the order and `ck_purchase_order_approved` refuses an approved, sent or received order at or above it without an approval request. A refused or withdrawn order goes back to DRAFT. **(b) Units:** an order line takes an optional `uom` (the unit it is bought by, a 50 kg bag); `qtyOrdered` and `unitPricePesewas` are in that unit and the line also stores `qty_ordered_base` from `MasterDataRefs.toBaseIn` (A-163). Received and invoiced quantities are in the base unit, as goods receipts book them. An invoice line takes its order line's unit. **(c) Receipts:** INV publishes `inv.GoodsReceiptPosted` once per posted receipt; PUR keeps each order line's `qty_received` from it in a transactional subscriber (a module keeping its own aggregate consistent with a lower module's fact, ADR-0010), refusing the whole receipt with 422 `RECEIPT_EXCEEDS_ORDER` past ordered plus tolerance or `RECEIPT_ORDER_MISMATCH` when the line is not on an open order of that supplier. Accepted quantity counts; rejected does not. A receipt's value is not checked against the order price (B8 posts 2010 at the order value carried on the invoice event). **(d) Invoiced quantity is derived, not stored:** the data model's `qty_invoiced` column is replaced by the sum of matched, approved and paid invoices' base quantities, so nothing can drift. **(e) The match:** price within 1 percent either way (SA-08), quantity no more than received less what other live invoices hold (lines of one invoice count against each other); a product line with no order line, a product that differs from its order line, and a levy charged differently from the calculator's amount each mismatch; a pure expense line needs none (`NOT_REQUIRED`). Approval matches again and refuses a mismatch with `THREE_WAY_MATCH_FAILED`; so does a payment allocation, by the same code (FR-PUR-010), not API.md's older `INVOICE_NOT_MATCHED`. The approved-variance route of FR-PUR-010 is not built: a mismatch is resolved by correcting the invoice or receiving the goods. **(f) Invoices:** only kind `INVOICE` (a credit note is refused with `CREDIT_NOTE_NOT_AVAILABLE` until credit notes are built); tax-exclusive prices only; the due date defaults to the invoice date until supplier terms exist; an invoice with no order names its branch (`branchId`, added); an optional `statedGrossPesewas` must equal the lines; a number is unique per supplier among invoices not cancelled. **(g) Orders:** tax-inclusive orders are refused (`TAX_INCLUSIVE_NOT_AVAILABLE`); sending records `sent_at` and publishes `pur.PurchaseOrderSent` for NTF (B11) but renders no document until document rendering exists (B8); numbers stay null until numbering (B8). **(h) Payments:** allocations are written when the payment is prepared (LEDGER, append-only); those of a REJECTED or CANCELLED payment stop counting; a payment is always approved by a different person (`SUPPLIER_PAYMENT`, no threshold) and pays only APPROVED invoices; `paid_pesewas` moves only when the payment is marked paid, by trigger. `bankAccountId` is refused (`BANK_ACCOUNT_NOT_AVAILABLE`) until `fin.bank_account` exists (B8 adds the column and its key, DATA_MODEL.md Migrations); the remittance advice waits for document rendering. **(i) Grants:** orders: Owner, Manager, Storekeeper make and submit; Owner and Manager approve, send, close and cancel; the Accountant reads. Invoices: Owner and Accountant enter, match and approve; the Manager reads. Payments: Owner and Accountant prepare and mark paid; Owner and Manager approve. Both approval policies ask for a fresh step-up. | Owner (a, i); accountant (e, f, h) | B8 (postings), B07 continued (variance approval, credit notes, terms) | Owner, 2026-11-15: approved for now on conditions (a step-up, audited setting and an order-splitting check, A-168); the grants approved subject to the payout rule (A-169 to A-171); per-role limits later |

| A-166 | 2026-11-15 | How B07 continued builds the supplier register and requisitions (FR-PUR-001 to FR-PUR-004), taking the most conservative reading where the documents leave a choice. **(a) Every payout change needs approval, the first set included:** any `payoutDetails` on `updateSupplierProfile` answers 202 `SUPPLIER_PAYOUT_CHANGE`, and the terms sent in the same request wait with it (the contract's 202 says nothing takes effect). | A-169 | 2026-11-15 | **Owner's decision: no single role both changes a supplier's payout details and approves or records a payment to that supplier.** Payout side: asking for a change (`pur.supplier.payout_change`, split out of `pur.supplier.update`) and approving one (`pur.supplier.payout_approve`); payment side: `pur.supplier_payment.create`, `.approve`, `.pay`. | A-171 | 2026-11-15 | **Owner's answer to the B07 continued stop: cross-approval and a person-level bar.** **(a)** At role level the Owner holds `pur.supplier.payout_change` and the payment approve and record permissions, as ADR-0043 requires; ADR-0043 and A-120 are not changed. **(b) Cross-approval:** a payout change made by the Owner is approved by a Manager or an Accountant, and one made by a Manager or an Accountant by the Owner (or the other), always a different person; so the Manager and the Accountant gain `pur.supplier.payout_change` and `pur.supplier.payout_approve`, and the Manager gains `pur.supplier.payout_read` (to check a change it approves) and `pur.supplier_payment.create` (the smallest team: the Owner makes the change, the Manager approves it, the Owner prepares the first payment, the Manager approves it), migration 0106. Held in the domain (`judgeFirstPaymentApprover`) and by the database (`pur.supplier_payment_first_approver`, HK010, on the approving update's `updated_by`, migration 0105). **(d)** The role-level proof that remains: no role outside the Owner, the Manager and the Accountant holds any payout permission, so none outside them holds a payout and a payment permission together. | A-172 | 2026-11-15 | **How B07 continued 1b builds supplier credit notes and the approved variance (FR-PUR-010, FR-PUR-011), taking the most conservative reading.** Both change what a supplier is owed, so both wait for a different person through IAM's approval mechanism (ADR-0017), whatever the amount, with a fresh step-up. **(a) Credit notes:** `createSupplierInvoice` with kind `CREDIT_NOTE` names an **APPROVED** invoice of the same supplier (one not yet approved is corrected instead; a PAID one has nothing left, and a refund owed by the supplier waits for the ledger, BL-059) and takes its order and branch. The cap counts every credit note against the invoice that is not cancelled, a waiting one included, and every live payment allocation: credits plus payments never exceed the gross, in the domain (`judgeCreditNote`) and the database (`ck_supplier_credit_note_cap`, deferred, the invoice row locked). A credit is DRAFT, then `submitSupplierCreditNote` (new) answers 202 `SUPPLIER_CREDIT_NOTE`, approved by a holder of `pur.supplier_invoice.approve` (the Owner and the Accountant, as B7 granted) who neither submitted nor entered it (IAM's check, the domain and `ck_supplier_invoice_credit_approved`). Only on approval is it credited (`credited_pesewas` on the invoice, kept by trigger and checked at commit), do goods go back and is the ledger event `pur.SupplierCreditNoteApproved` published. An approved credit note is never cancelled; an invoice with a live credit note is not cancelled. A payment pays only what credits leave; an invoice is PAID once a payment was made and payments and credits reach its gross (credited in full with nothing paid, it stays APPROVED with nothing outstanding). **(b) Goods sent back:** a credit line may name `returnFromLocationId` (a physical location of the branch) and `returnStockUnitId` (the lot or animal), with a `RETURN` reason; on approval each moves its base quantity as INV's `SUPPLIER_RETURN` movement to the branch's virtual SUPPLIER location through `Stock.postIn`, at the cost the pool gives (INV refuses what is not on hand). **(c) The approved variance:** `requestSupplierInvoiceVariance` (new) on a MISMATCH invoice answers 202 `SUPPLIER_INVOICE_VARIANCE` and records the match as it stands; a variance covers a price outside the tolerance or a levy charged differently, **never goods not received, a line with no order line or another product** (422 `VARIANCE_NOT_ALLOWED`, and `pur.supplier_invoice_variance_cover` in the database), which are resolved by receiving or correcting. The approver holds the new `pur.supplier_invoice.variance_approve` (the Owner and the Manager, who approve payments; not the Accountant, who asks) and is never the person who asked nor the person who entered the invoice (422 `INVOICE_MAKER_CANNOT_APPROVE`, and `ck_supplier_invoice_variance` on the row). On approval the invoice is APPROVED with match `VARIANCE_APPROVED` (its lines keep their MISMATCH results) and its ledger event names the variance; the approval is refused if the match changed since it was asked (`MATCH_CHANGED`). A waiting credit or variance freezes the document (422 `APPROVAL_PENDING`); a refused or withdrawn one leaves it to be corrected | Owner (grants, approver roles); accountant (a, the PAID rule) | B8 (postings), B12 (exception report) | Open |
| A-173 | 2026-11-15 | **How supplier scores are computed (FR-PUR-014).** Computed when asked, for one calendar month (`periodStart` must be a first of the month), from PUR's own facts, never stored: the `pur.supplier_score` projection of the data model is not built. **Price:** the supplier's net on approved and paid invoice lines with a product, against the same quantities at the farm's average price for each product in the month (all suppliers): 100 less the percentage over it, never above 100 nor below 0 (the SRS's "against the average paid"; the data model's "invoice against order price" is not used). **Lead time:** the share of the supplier's receipt lines that arrived on or before the promised day, the order's expected date or else its date plus the supplier's lead time; a receipt with no promise is left out (binary on time, not graded). **Quality:** the accepted share of what was received at the gate (`rejectionPercent` is the rest). **Rejection:** 100 less the share of the month's accepted quantity sent back on approved credit notes (`returnedPercent`), so quality and rejection measure different things, as the SRS example (quality 100, rejection 95) requires. The weights are four farm-wide integer settings (`pur.supplier_score.*_weight`, 30, 25, 25, 20), changed through `updateSetting`, and the score divides by their sum so a weight changed alone keeps it within 0 to 100; a component without facts, or all weights zero, gives no score. Receipt facts are recorded in PUR's existing transactional subscriber to `inv.GoodsReceiptPosted` (`pur.supplier_receipt_line`), not the asynchronous `inv.GoodsReceived` the PUR card planned; receipts posted before migration 0108 have none. Suppliers are farm-wide, so any grant of `pur.supplier.read` reads every score | Owner (component definitions, weights) | B12 (reporting may store them) | Open |

The confirmer is a holder of the new `liv.mortality.confirm`, granted to the Owner and the Manager only, never the Storekeeper, whom abuse case 2 names and who reads no approval inbox; the new `liv.mortality.post` (Owner, Manager, Storekeeper) is the direct write-off below the threshold that every reporter but the Farm hand keeps. RETURNED is final for that record; the animal may be reported again with a new photograph. The Farm hand reads no death record, so it learns of the return from the notification B11 sends on that event. **(e) Reuse:** the photograph's SHA-256 is copied onto the record at report (`photo_sha256`; records before migration 0113 have none); a second record with the same bytes publishes `liv.MortalityPhotoReused` (abuse case 2) for B12; exact bytes only (BL-061). **(f) Tag lookup (CR-021):** `getCareAnimalByTag` at `/care/animal-tags/{tagNo}` (a path under `/care/animals/` is ambiguous with `{id}/move`), under `liv.care.read` (Owner, Manager, Farm hand) rather than CR-021's "any of the record permissions": the role already reads the care register, and one permission keeps the read deny-by-default; exact, case-sensitive, ALIVE only, otherwise 404 | Owner (who confirms; the matrix); security (the hash's limit) | B06r; B11 (the maker's notification); B12 (the exception items) | Open |

While a reversal waits the order refuses another (`REVERSAL_PENDING`) and packing; a rejected one frees the order. **Below the threshold** the required reason posts it at once. **Exception report:** every posted reversal publishes `prc.ProcessingOrderReversed` with its value, threshold, approval and reason and abuse case 29 (new). **Refused outright** in the domain and, for a pack PICKED, SOLD or WRITTEN_OFF, in the database (`ck_processing_reversal_unsold`, 23514); a lot moved or sold from the cold room is refused by INV's own negative-stock check on the reversing movement. | A-190 | 2026-11-16 | **Manual journals (FR-FIN-008, ADR-0003's "As entered", ADR-0017), each the most conservative reading, changeable as data or by a forward migration.** <ul><li>**(a) Who:** the Owner and the Accountant each hold `fin.journal.read`, `fin.journal.create` and `fin.journal.approve` (step-up, on the permission and on the `MANUAL_JOURNAL` policy, whatever the amount), so the smallest team that keeps the books, one Owner and one Accountant, can post only by one approving the other's journal; a farm with a single Owner and no Accountant or second Owner can never post its own (ADR-0017's consequence). | A-194 | 2026-11-16 | **The business's bank accounts and the supplier payment's account, each the most conservative reading.** <ul><li>**(a) Who:** the Owner and the Accountant hold `fin.bank_account.read` and `fin.bank_account.manage` (they prepare supplier payments, A-164); no other role. `fin.bank_account.manage` requires step-up, so **renaming and deactivating need a fresh step-up too**, not only adding (the contract marked only `createBankAccount`; CR-007's rule makes the marker equal the permission's, so `updateBankAccount` gains `x-requires-step-up` in 1.36.0).</li><li>**(b) The number:** a bank number is 6 to 20 digits (spaces and dashes dropped, as PUR's payout details), a wallet number a Ghana mobile number kept as +233 and nine digits; only the last four digits leave FIN. Names are unique among accounts, ignoring case and outer spaces.</li><li>**(c) The ledger:** the GL account follows the kind, 1100 for `BANK` and 1110 for `MOBILE_MONEY` (`ck_bank_account_gl`), never chosen by the client; each account gets its own `BANK_ACCOUNT` subledger party in the transaction that adds it (`ck_bank_account_party`), named by its name then, so the accountant can reclass A-185's "Bank account not yet named" postings to it by manual journal before anything else has posted to it. A rename does not rename the party (a snapshot for statements).</li><li>**(d) Supplier payments:** `bankAccountId` stays optional (A-185 posts a payment naming none as before); one named must be an active account of the method's kind (a bank for `BANK` and `CHEQUE`, a wallet for `MOBILE_MONEY`, none for `CASH`), else 422 `BANK_ACCOUNT_NOT_AVAILABLE` (also for an unknown id); the database holds that a cash payment names none and that the account never changes once written; the kind check is in the application only, because a PUR trigger may not read `fin.bank_account` (MODULE_BOUNDARIES section 1). The approval's payload hash includes the account only when one is named, so payments prepared before 1.36.0 keep their hash. `pur.SupplierPaymentMade` v1 gains the optional `bankAccountId`; FIN credits that account's party, and reads an event without it as A-185.</li><li>**(e) Seed:** one bank current account and one MTN merchant wallet, SEED, with numbers that are zeros but their last digits; nothing posts, so the seeded day's trial balance is unchanged.</li></ul> | Accountant (b to e), Owner (a) | B08 continued 1c | Open |
| A-195 | 2026-11-16 | **The financial statements (FR-FIN-011 to FR-FIN-013, ADR-0058), each the most conservative reading, changeable as data or code with no contract change.** <ul><li>**(a) Who:** the new permission `fin.report.read` is granted to the Owner and the Accountant only (the Manager's report access comes with the reports, A-188 d); without `branchId` a statement covers every branch, as the journal reads do; with it, the reader must hold the permission for that branch.</li><li>**(b) Trial balance:** rows are accounts in code order, those with no figure left out unless `includeZero`; `totalDebitPesewas` and `totalCreditPesewas` are the range's debits and credits over the whole filter; `balanced` also needs the closing balances to net to zero and the signed sum to `to` to be exactly zero.</li><li>**(c) Profit and loss:** rows per account; income as net credits (a contra account such as 4200 to 4220 a negative row), cost of sales (5xxx, own production 5210 a negative row) and expenses as net debits; year close entries left out.</li><li>**(d) Balance sheet:** rows per account (not the contract example's groups, which the frontend can build from the account codes); accumulated depreciation a negative asset row; the earnings not yet closed to 3100 one equity row with no account; `LIABILITIES_AND_EQUITY` their sum.</li><li>**(e) Cash flow:** direct method over accounts 1000 to 1130 (by code, so a cash account the accountant adds in that range counts); sections `OPENING_CASH`, `OPERATING`, `INVESTING`, `FINANCING`, `NET_CHANGE`, `CLOSING_CASH`; a counterpart with no `cash_flow_category` is operating; a move between two cash accounts is no flow.</li><li>**(f) Projection:** `fin.period_balance` is trusted only for LOCKED and CLOSED periods (ADR-0058); open periods are read from their lines, and the nightly refresh of open periods serves the drift check only.</li></ul> | Accountant (b to f), Owner (a) | B08 continued 1c2 | Open |

A per-kilogram line needs its weight and the weight's source; a serialised product is one named animal to a line; a lot-tracked product names its lot; a line in another unit converts through `MasterDataRefs.toBaseIn`.</li><li>**(d) Lines and stock:** every line of a COMPLETED order has its stock movement or its stock exception (`ck_sales_order_settled_lines`), so a product kept out of stock (`NON_STOCK`) is not sold on an order line (`PRODUCT_NOT_STOCKED`).
