| A-374 | 2026-11-24 | **NTF's first pass: the customer's messages, sent once to the order's own contact** (B11 continued 10, ADR-0096). (a) **Two steps.** NTF's outbox consumer `ntf.customer-messages` (never a transactional subscriber) queues one message per event, keyed by the event, its template, its channel and its order (`uq_message_idempotency`), with its inbox row; the worker's `message-send` job (every 15 s) claims due messages (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, a two-minute lease with a token), calls the provider outside any transaction with the message id as its idempotency key, and records the attempt only while its claim holds. Three attempts with backoff and jitter, then `FAILED` (published `ntf.MessageFailed`); a permanent refusal fails at once; the first temporary failure in a pass hands the rest back (the circuit). A manager's retry queues a `FAILED` message again; a `SUPPRESSED` one never. (b) **No address is stored** (the data model's `address_enc` is not built): the number is read from ORD (`Orders.messageContactsIn`: the order's contact, else its delivery's recipient) when the message is queued and again when it is sent, never from an event's payload; the log keeps it masked (`+23324*****56`, FR-NTF-007) and an HMAC of it under a key derived from the lookup key, for the recipient's limit: at most ten messages an hour (`RATE_LIMITED`, `ntf.MessageSuppressed`, abuse case 58). An order with no number gives a `SUPPRESSED` `NO_ADDRESS` message. (c) **Templates:** a code NTF sends, in its own category, naming only that code's merge fields; no link typed into the text (`LINK_NOT_ALLOWED`: a link a message needs is filled in by the server, ADR-0074); a change is a new DRAFT version activated by a second person (never its drafter, held by the database), which retires the one it replaces; a version's text never changes. The Owner and the Manager draft and activate; the Accountant and Sales read them. (d) **Channels and provider:** only SMS, through the platform's `generic-http` adapter (`capture` sends nothing), so every customer message goes by SMS until preferences arrive; the provider's delivery reports come to `receiveMessagingWebhook` as `GENERIC_HTTP` (added to its enum), signed HMAC-SHA256 hex over the raw body under `SMS_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (optional config: without it every report is refused 401 and a sent message stays `SENT`; B13 supplies it with the provider's key). `HUBTEL`, `META_WHATSAPP` and `SENDGRID` answer 404 until their adapters exist. A STOP reply is logged `IGNORED` until the opt-out list is built (B11 continued 11). (e) The seven customer templates (dispatched, collected, on the way, rescheduled, cancelled, delivered, part delivered) are seeded active by migration 0335 in English; the delivery-day messages are exempt from quiet hours (FR-NTF-006), so none waits. (f) **Exactly once** rests on the database key for queuing and, for a worker that died after the provider took the message, on the provider honouring the idempotency key (the `generic-http` adapter sends it as `Idempotency-Key` and `reference`); B13 confirms the chosen provider does. | Owner | B11 continued 10 | Open |
| A-375 | 2026-11-24 | **B11 continued 10 split at a sub-domain boundary of NTF.** Built the module with templates, the message log with each attempt and the provider's delivery reports, the sender and the customer's messages from ORD's and DEL's events, to their own gate (A-374, contract 2.39.0, migrations 0335 to 0337), and stopped before preferences, opt-outs, quiet hours, triggers, in-app notifications, the document sends, the remittance advice, abandoned carts, the returned death report, CR-033 point 3's receipt SMS, the tracking SMS, the reminder templates and their delivery per channel, CR-039, the reused-photograph match and the UAT dry runs, which would not reach their gate in the context left. **B11 continued 11** (`b11c11-notifications`), inserted before B12 (ADR-0026 Amendments, the openers file, the reference table, the lane B order), builds them. | Parent session | B11 continued 11 | Open |
| A-376 | 2026-11-24 | **NTF's second pass: triggers, preferences, opt-outs, quiet hours, reminders and in-app notifications** (B11 continued 11, ADR-0097). (a) **Which messages an opt-out or a preference stops:** a `REMINDER` (the payment reminders) or `MARKETING` message is suppressed on a channel the number opted out of (`OPTED_OUT`) or the recipient turned off (`PREFERENCE_OFF`); a `TRANSACTIONAL` message (a receipt, a dispatch, a delivery, a collection: what the law or the owner requires) and an `OPERATIONAL` one to staff are never suppressed by either: a preference only orders their channels, and with every channel off they go by the trigger's first with an adapter. (b) **Quiet hours** (SA-09) delay reminders and marketing only, to 07:00 Africa/Accra or the end of the recipient's own window, on the minute (`DEFERRED_QUIET_HOURS` with `send_after`, never dropped); transactional and operational messages, among them every delivery-day message, go at once; quiet hours are refused on a transactional or operational preference (`QUIET_HOURS_NOT_DELAYED`). The sender checks the opt-outs and the quiet hours again just before it sends, so a reminder waiting for 07:00 when a STOP arrives is suppressed, never sent, and a manager's retry at night waits. A channel with no adapter (WhatsApp, email) is chosen only when nothing with one is left, so its messages fail visibly; the fall-back to the next channel after three failures waits for a second adapter (BL-117). (c) **Triggers:** every message NTF sends has one, seeded by migration 0338 (eleven: the seven customer messages, the three reminders, the returned death report); turned off, it queues nothing new and changes nothing made; a change is its channels or on and off, at the version read, by holders of `ntf.template.manage` (the Owner, the Manager), audited, no second person (a trigger is not on the maker-checker list); its template is fixed (`TEMPLATE_FIXED`). A reminder goes only by the channels its dunning level names that its trigger also allows. (d) **Opt-outs** are kept on the number (its mask and keyed hash, `ntf.opt_out`, LEDGER), not on the customer record, so a guest's STOP counts and an opt-out outlives the record; a signed reply whose first word is STOP, STOPALL, UNSUBSCRIBE, CANCEL, END or QUIT is an opt-out of SMS at once, START or UNSTOP an opt-in, once by its id; any other reply is logged `IGNORED`; a reply without its id or number is logged malformed. A preference screen does not undo a STOP (only the number's own START does). `listOptOuts` is read by holders of `ntf.message.read` (the Owner, the Manager) at any branch: a number is no branch's. Staff SMS is not offered (no staff number is kept): staff get `IN_APP` only. (e) **In-app notifications** are messages on `IN_APP` to a `STAFF` recipient, with a title (an in-app template's `subject`) and a console path (never a URL); the sender writes the notification once (`uq_in_app_notification_message`); a recipient lists and marks only their own, another's is 404; read once. The first is the death report sent back by the scan (`MORTALITY_RETURNED`, OPERATIONAL, to the record's own reporter read through LIV). `markNotificationRead` takes no idempotency key, as the contract planned: it is idempotent by itself. (f) **Preferences:** a customer's on the customer record its sign-in belongs to (404 when none is linked), staff's and investors' on their principal; replaced whole, in order within each category; none set means every channel accepted with the default quiet hours. (g) **Reminders** are written from the dunning notice as WHL holds it (`Dunning.noticesIn`) and the customer's own number (MDM's `customerContactsIn`), never the event's copy; a level naming a code that is not `PAYMENT_REMINDER_1` to `3` sends nothing (making a level pick a listed template is B11 continued 12's). | Owner | B13 | Open |
| A-377 | 2026-11-24 | **B11 continued 11 split at its first sub-domain boundary.** Built preferences, opt-outs (a STOP reply recorded at once), quiet hours, triggers and in-app notifications to their own gate (A-376, ADR-0097, migration 0338, contract 2.40.0), with the payment reminders and the returned death report as the first reminder and the first in-app notification they act on, and stopped before the document sends (`sendBillingDocument`, `sendStatement`), the remittance advice, CR-033 point 3's receipt SMS, the tracking SMS, abandoned carts, a dunning level picking a listed template and each reminder's delivery shown on its notice per channel, CR-039, the reused-photograph match and the UAT dry runs: the receipt SMS touches POS's receipt links (the parent's leave needed) and the rest would not reach its gate in the context left. **B11 continued 12** (`b11c12-notifications`), inserted before B12 (ADR-0026 Amendments, the openers file, the reference table, the lane B order, B12's reused-photograph line). | A-349 | 2026-11-24 | **The messages that carry a link and the receipt SMS (ADR-0084; the parent session's leave in POS of 2026-11-24), each the most conservative option.** (a) A link is made when the message is sent, by the record's own module, in the sender's own transaction, and another for each attempt. The stored text keeps `{{link}}` unfilled, so no token is in the outbox, the message, an attempt, an audit row or a log. A record with no link to give is `SUPPRESSED` `NO_LINK`, published with abuse case 60. With no shop address (`RECEIPT_LINK_BASE_URL`), the message fails visibly. (b) Links are built under the shop's address the till's receipt links already use: `/track/<token>`, `/r/<token>`, `/cart`. The storefront serves `/r/` already; `/track/` and `/cart` are the frontend's to route (a note in the report). (c) The receipt SMS goes only for a counter sale a till made and synced (`ord.OrderCompleted`, `POS`, offline), once, to the order's own contact, which is the `customerPhone` the till sent, read again at send. It is transactional: a STOP does not stop it, but the hourly limit does. A live counter sale sends none, since the till prints and shows its receipt. (d) Its link is an `rcp_` token bound to the sale's till link (`pos.receipt_message_link`): it answers exactly the till's receipt, expires with it, and is revoked with it. A sale whose till token was refused, or which sent none, gets no receipt by message, because a receipt without the till's tenders would misstate how the buyer paid (BL-118). (e) `SyncResultItem.receiptMessage` is not added: a replay is answered from the stored sync row, so the field would change POS's existing sync answer (BL-119). (f) The tracking SMS goes when an online order is placed, to the order's contact, as a transactional message. | Owner (b, c, d); parent session (the POS leave) | B11 continued 13 (the frontend routes) | Open |

| A-294 | 2026-11-24 | **An accepted quote's hold ends 7 days after acceptance (the owner, 2026-11-24, verbatim: "An accepted quote's hold ends 7 days after acceptance."; A-310 (b) amended; migration 0351).** (a) Measured from the acceptance instant (`quo.quote_acceptance.accepted_at`) by the application clock (ADR-0067): at 7 days exactly it has ended, a millisecond before it has not. (e) A conversion and the job at the same instant each lock the quote's row (`FOR UPDATE`, the job `SKIP LOCKED`), so the quote is either converted with its hold or released, never both (tested). (f) The 7 days are the owner's number, held as a constant, not a setting. (g) A buyer who comes back gets a new quote. | Owner | B11u continued | Open |

(b) The rest is released as before; what nothing held still waits for stock (the backorder is confirmed at once, as before). (c) The backorder's hold has no expiry; it ends when the backorder is dispatched, collected or cancelled. | A-350 | 2026-11-24 | **A quoted line booked at its list (the owner, 2026-11-23, UAT scenario 2 item 1; B11u, ADR-0098, migration 0348).** (a) The list is the one the quote line recorded at issue (`quo.quote_line.resolved_unit_price_pesewas`, re-priced at every issue since B10), never the engine at conversion, so a price list changed after issue never moves the booking. (c) A line naming a weight band and no animal is priced at the band's lowest weight, which the engine maps to that band (half-open, FR-MDM-005): it records that band's list and floor, and a quoted price below the band's floor now needs the margin approval, LOUD (it issued before). (d) The order line records the quoted price as agreed, the list as resolved, the floor as the lowest of the engine's floor, the list and the quoted price, the ceiling as the higher of the list and the quoted price (the band holds both, so the line needs no second approval: the margin step approved it), and the difference of the two prices each extended once, half-up, as its variance, under `QUOTED_PRICE`, the server's own reason in a domain of its own (no seller's list offers it, and the sale path and createOrder refuse it, since they take only `PRICE_OVERRIDE`). | A-351 | 2026-11-24 | **A quote holds its count, and its animals are named at picking (the owner, 2026-11-23, items 2 and 4; B11u, ADR-0098, migration 0349; replaces A-309 (f)'s "an unnamed animal is held by nobody").** (a) A line of animals naming none holds that many animals at issue, oldest first, capped at what is free, never below zero (a balance below zero is nothing, ADR-0081 never online), each to the quote's validity; a line naming a band holds only animals whose last weighing is in that band, and an animal never weighed is not held for it. | A-380 | 2026-11-24 | **Capital as built in B12 (FR-CAP-001, FR-CAP-003 to FR-CAP-005, ADR-0028, ADR-0100).** (a) **Units are never rounded:** a contribution or withdrawal issues or redeems exactly the amount over the unit price, to four places; an amount that does not buy whole ten-thousandths of a unit is refused (`UNITS_NOT_EXACT`), and the database holds it (`ck_capital_transaction_units`). (b) **The unit price is GHS 100.00 unless given** (UAT section 3.5 and the contract's default; A-031's GHS 1.00 was the brief's example). (c) **A contribution needs no approval; a withdrawal always does** (FR-CAP-003 and FR-CAP-004 as written; the opener's "with approval" read as the withdrawal's). (d) **A value date after today is refused; one in a locked, closed or missing period is refused** (`PERIOD_NOT_OPEN`); **one before the ledger's first period posts on that period's first day** (the books begin 1 January 2026; UAT section 3.5's 2025 contributions), its units still counting from the value date: the money is an opening balance of the books, the units are the investor's from the day they paid. (e) **Investors are numbered INV-001, INV-002 in order** (UAT section 3.5), never reused, ties broken by the number's value. (f) **Not built in B12, B12 continued's:** payout details and the identity number (sealed, FR-CAP-002), investor documents, the agreement file, the portal login, return metrics (FR-CAP-014), the statement (FR-CAP-013), loans and grants (FR-CAP-015); `createInvestor` and `updateInvestor` refuse those fields (`FIELD_NOT_YET_SUPPORTED`), writing nothing. (g) The seed's investors, contributions and the trading summary of July to September 2026 (net profit GHS 41,666.70) are historical fixed dates, as UAT section 3.5 fixes them; they never expire, and only `pnpm db:seed` and the tests that read them load them (`seedUnits(env, { investors: true })`). | Owner, Accountant | B12 continued | Open |
| A-381 | 2026-11-24 | **Who keeps the investor books (B12, ADR-0017, ADR-0100).** (a) **They are kept at the head office branch:** every CAP permission is checked at the head office (MDM's first live branch), so a grant at another branch, an Owner's included, reads and changes nothing. (b) **The Accountant and the Owner hold the CAP permissions; no other staff role reads investor data, the Manager included** (FR-CAP-001: confidential). The Owner holds the maker's permissions too, because IAM lets a granter grant only what they hold; the Owner is the only checker (`cap.capital.approve`, `cap.distribution.approve`), and whoever asked for a withdrawal or drafted a run never approves it (IAM, the application and the database). (c) **A fresh step-up for changing the register, recording capital, posting a run and recording a payment to an investor** (the contract planned the first three; the fourth is added as money leaving the farm, abuse case 68), as well as for both approvals. | Owner | B12 continued | Open |
| A-382 | 2026-11-24 | **A submitted distribution run is never edited (FR-CAP-010, ADR-0100).** "Editing a submitted run voids the approval" is held by refusing every edit once a run is submitted (`RUN_NOT_EDITABLE`; the database's `ck_distribution_run_frozen`): the approval always decides exactly what was submitted, and the schedule archived at submission (`DISTRIBUTION_RUN`, one revision, `ck_archived_document_revision`) is the one approved. A run that must change is rejected by the checker, or withdrawn by its maker through IAM, and a new run is drafted (a rejected run's period is free again). A draft may be recomputed any number of times; each computation is kept. | Owner, Accountant | B12 continued | Open |
